\displaystyle\ll\lambda^{1-n}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda)d{\boldsymbol{x}}\ll\lambda^{2}\leq\lambda A^{-k}.

Case (b): Assume that
A^{-k}<\lambda\leq A^{-1/(2n)}.
Let
\varphi\in C^{\infty}
be such that
\text{supp}(\varphi)\subseteq[-5,5]
,
\varphi>0
and
\frac{d^{m}\varphi(y)}{dy^{m}}\ll 1,
where the implicit constant may depend on
m.
Then, we find by the definition of
C_{P}(A)
that

\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda)\ll\int_{B_{N}(N)}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\\
P({\boldsymbol{b}})=0\end{subarray}}\biggl(\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}-b_{i})\biggr)\varphi\biggl(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{\lambda^{2}}\biggr)\biggl(\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\varphi\left(\frac{\partial_{x_{i}}f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{2\lambda N}\right)\biggr)d{\boldsymbol{a}}.

For simplicity, here and throughout this proof, we write

w({\boldsymbol{c}},{\boldsymbol{x}})=\varphi\biggl(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{c}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{\lambda^{2}}\biggr)\biggl(\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\varphi\left(\frac{\partial_{x_{i}}f_{{\boldsymbol{c}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{2\lambda N}\right)\biggr),

with
{\boldsymbol{c}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}.

By orthogonality, one deduces that

\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda)\ll\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\Sigma({\boldsymbol{a}})d{\boldsymbol{a}},

where

\Sigma({\boldsymbol{a}})=\int_{0}^{1}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\end{subarray}}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}-b_{i})d\alpha.

Recalling the definition
(\ref{6.102})
of
\mathfrak{M}_{\delta}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\delta}
, one finds that

(5.108)

\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda)\ll\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\left(\Sigma_{1}({\boldsymbol{a}})+\Sigma_{2}({\boldsymbol{a}})\right)d{\boldsymbol{a}},

where

\Sigma_{1}({\boldsymbol{a}})=\int_{\mathfrak{M}_{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\end{subarray}}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}-b_{i})d\alpha

and

\Sigma_{2}({\boldsymbol{a}})=\int_{\mathfrak{m}_{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\end{subarray}}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}-b_{i})d\alpha.

We first analyze the quantity

\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\Sigma_{1}({\boldsymbol{a}})d{\boldsymbol{a}}

Since
\text{supp}(\varphi)\subseteq[-5,5]
, we observe that for fixed
{\boldsymbol{a}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\end{subarray}}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}-b_{i})\ll 1.

Hence, on noting that
\text{mes}(\mathfrak{M}_{1/(6n)})\leq A^{1/(3n)-k},
we obtain that

\Sigma_{1}\ll\text{mes}(\mathfrak{M}_{1/(6n)})\leq A^{1/(3n)-k}.

Then, on noting that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})d{\boldsymbol{a}}\ll\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in B_{N}(N)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} |f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq 5\lambda^{2},\ \|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 10n^{1/2}\lambda N\end{aligned}\right\}\right),

it follows by applying Lemma
2.7
with
5\lambda^{2}
and
10n^{1/2}\lambda N
in place of
\lambda^{2}
and
2\lambda
that

(5.109)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\Sigma_{1}({\boldsymbol{a}})d{\boldsymbol{a}}

\displaystyle\ll A^{1/(3n)-k}\cdot\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in B_{N}(N)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} |f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq 5\lambda^{2},\ \|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 10n^{1/2}\lambda N\end{aligned}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\ll A^{1/(3n)-k}\lambda^{n+1}\ll\lambda^{n}A^{-k},

where we have used the upper bound
\lambda\leq A^{-1/(2n)}.
Here, one easily infers that Lemma
2.7
is applicable in
(\ref{6.114})
, since
10n^{1/2}N
and
5
are
O(1).

We turn to estimate

(5.110)

\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\Sigma_{2}({\boldsymbol{a}})d{\boldsymbol{a}}.

By using the identity

\varphi(y)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi)e(\xi y)d\xi,

we find that
(\ref{6.115})
is seen to be

(5.111)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{0})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{i})\int_{\mathfrak{m}_{1/(6n)}}\Xi_{0}(\alpha,\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})d\alpha d\xi_{0}d\boldsymbol{\xi},

where

\Xi_{0}(\alpha,\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})=\displaystyle\sum_{\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))\int_{B_{N}(N)}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}-b_{i})e\biggl(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\xi_{0}}{\lambda^{2}}\biggr)e\biggl(\frac{\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}}{2\lambda N}\biggr)d{\boldsymbol{a}}.

By the change of variable
{\boldsymbol{a}}={\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}+{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}
, we see that
\Xi_{0}(\alpha,\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})
is

(5.112)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))\int_{B_{N}(N)-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}^{\prime})e\left(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}+{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\xi_{0}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)e\left(\frac{\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}+{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}}{2\lambda N}\right)d{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\sum_{\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))\int_{B_{N}(N/2)}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}^{\prime})e\left(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}+{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\xi_{0}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)e\left(\frac{\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}+{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}}{2\lambda N}\right)d{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=S_{1}(\alpha,\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})\cdot S_{2}(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi}),

where

S_{1}(\alpha,\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})=\displaystyle\sum_{\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))e\left(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\xi_{0}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)e\left(\frac{\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}}{2\lambda N}\right)

and

S_{2}(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})=\int_{B_{N}(N/2)}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\varphi(Aa_{i}^{\prime})e\left(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\xi_{0}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)e\left(\frac{\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}}{2\lambda N}\right)d{\boldsymbol{a}}^{\prime},

and we have used the fact that

\text{supp}(\varphi)\subseteq[-5,5]\ \text{and}\ \|{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1.

Meanwhile, since
P({\boldsymbol{b}})
is a non-singular polynomial of degree
k
, by the Weyl type estimate over minor arcs [
References
, Lemma 3.6], we have

\int_{\mathfrak{m}_{1/(6n)}}S_{1}(\alpha,\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})d\alpha\ll A^{N-N/(6n(k-1)2^{k-1})+\epsilon}.

Furthermore, since
S_{2}(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})\leq A^{-N}
and
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{0})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{i})d\xi_{0}d\boldsymbol{\xi}\ll 1
, one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{0})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{i})S_{2}(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})d\xi_{0}d\boldsymbol{\xi}\ll A^{-N}.

Hence, we find from expressions
(\ref{6.116})
and
(\ref{6.117})
that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\Sigma_{2}({\boldsymbol{a}})d{\boldsymbol{a}}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{0})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{i})\int_{\mathfrak{m}_{1/(6n)}}S_{1}(\alpha,\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})\cdot S_{2}(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})d\alpha d\xi_{0}d\boldsymbol{\xi}\ll A^{-N/(6n(k-1)2^{k-1})+\epsilon}.

Noting a choice of
N
in the statement of Lemma
5.14
with
N>6n(n+1)k(k-1)2^{k-1}
and the condition
A^{-k}<\lambda\leq A^{-1/(2n)},
we find that

(5.113)

\int_{B_{N}(N)}w({\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\Sigma_{2}({\boldsymbol{a}})d{\boldsymbol{a}}\ll\lambda^{n}A^{-k}.

On substituting
(\ref{6.114})
and
(\ref{6.118})
into (
5.108
) and from that into
(\ref{6.111})
, we see that whenever
A^{-k}<\lambda\leq A^{-1/(2n)}
one has

(5.114)

\text{mes}\left(B_{N}^{(\lambda)}\right)\ll\lambda A^{-k}.

Case (c): Assume that
A^{-1/(2n)}<\lambda.
Recall the definition of
\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda),
that is

\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda)=\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in C_{P}(A)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} |f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq\lambda^{2},\ \|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 2\lambda N\end{aligned}\right\}\right).

Note that for given
{\boldsymbol{x}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{n-1}
and
{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{b}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}
with
\|{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}\|\leq A^{-1}
for sufficiently large
A>0
, it follows by applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that we have

|f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq\|{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}\|\|\nu_{d,n}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq\lambda^{2},

where we used
A^{-1/(2n)}<\lambda
for the second inequality.
Thus, whenever
|f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq\lambda^{2}
, we deduce by the triangle inequality that

|f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq|f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|+|f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq 2\lambda^{2}.

Similarly, for given
{\boldsymbol{x}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{n-1}
and
{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{b}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}
with
\|{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}\|\leq A^{-1}
for sufficiently large
A>0
, whenever
\|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 2\lambda N
, we have

\|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq\|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|+\|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 4\lambda N.

Hence, on recalling the definitions of
C_{P}(A)
and
w({\boldsymbol{c}},{\boldsymbol{x}})
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda)

\displaystyle\ll\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\\
P({\boldsymbol{b}})=0\end{subarray}}\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in B_{N}(N)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} &(i)\ |f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq\lambda^{2},\|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 2\lambda N\\
&(ii)\ \|{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}\|\leq A^{-1}\end{aligned}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\ll\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\\
P({\boldsymbol{b}})=0\end{subarray}}\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in B_{N}(N)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} &(i)\ |f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq 2\lambda^{2},\|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 4\lambda N\\
&(ii)\ \|{\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1}\|\leq A^{-1}\end{aligned}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\ll A^{-N}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\\
P({\boldsymbol{b}})=0\end{subarray}}w({\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1},{\boldsymbol{x}}).

By orthogonality and major and minor arcs dissection again, one sees that

(5.115)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}({\boldsymbol{x}},\lambda)\ll A^{-N}\int_{0}^{1}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A\end{subarray}}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))w({\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1},{\boldsymbol{x}})d\alpha=A^{-N}(\Xi_{1}+\Xi_{2}),

where

\Xi_{1}=\int_{\mathfrak{M}_{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))w({\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1},{\boldsymbol{x}})d\alpha

and

\Xi_{2}=\int_{\mathfrak{m}_{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|_{\infty}\leq A}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))w({\boldsymbol{b}}A^{-1},{\boldsymbol{x}})d\alpha.

By using the Fourier transform of
\varphi
, we find that

(5.116)

\Xi_{2}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{0})\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{i})\right)T(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})d\xi_{0}d\boldsymbol{\xi},

where

T(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})=\int_{\mathfrak{m}_{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\|{\boldsymbol{b}}\|\leq A}e(\alpha P({\boldsymbol{b}}))e\left(\frac{f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\xi_{0}}{A\lambda^{2}}\right)e\left(\frac{\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}}{2A\lambda N}\right)d\alpha.

Hence, since
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{0})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\varphi}(\xi_{i})d\xi_{0}d\boldsymbol{\xi}\ll 1
and
A^{-1/(2n)}<\lambda
, it follows by the Weyl type estimate over minor arcs for the exponential sum of the integrand in

T(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})
[
References
, Lemma 3.6] and our choice of
N
that

(5.117)

\Xi_{2}\ll A^{N-k}\lambda^{n}.

We turn to estimate
\Xi_{1}.

On recalling the definition of
\mathfrak{M}_{1/(6n)},
we find that

(5.118)

\Xi_{1}=\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq q\leq A^{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq a\leq q\\
(q,a)=1\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq{\boldsymbol{z}}\leq q\\
{\boldsymbol{z}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}e\left(\frac{a}{q}P({\boldsymbol{z}})\right)\int_{|\beta|\leq\frac{A^{1/(6n)}}{qA^{k}}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\boldsymbol{y}}\in B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\\
{\boldsymbol{y}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}h_{(\beta)}({\boldsymbol{y}})d\beta,

where
h_{(\beta)}({\boldsymbol{y}})=w\left(\frac{q{\boldsymbol{y}}+{\boldsymbol{z}}}{A},{\boldsymbol{x}}\right)e(\beta P(q{\boldsymbol{y}}+{\boldsymbol{z}}))
, and
B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}
is an
N
-dimensional box such that
{\boldsymbol{y}}\in B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}
implies
q{\boldsymbol{y}}+{\boldsymbol{z}}\in[-A,A]^{N}.
If
{\boldsymbol{x}}\in[0,1]^{N}
, then

h_{(\beta)}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}+{\boldsymbol{x}})=h_{(\beta)}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})+O\biggl(\max_{\boldsymbol{u}\in[0,1]^{N}}|\nabla h_{(\beta)}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}+\boldsymbol{u})|\biggr).

Hence, on noting that
\text{mes}(\text{supp}(B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}(\cdot)))\ll(A/q)^{N},
we see that

(5.119)

\biggl|\int_{B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}}h_{(\beta)}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})d\boldsymbol{\gamma}-\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\boldsymbol{y}}\in B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\\
{\boldsymbol{y}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}h_{(\beta)}({\boldsymbol{y}})\biggr|\ll(A/q)^{N}\max_{{\boldsymbol{y}}\in B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}}|\nabla h_{(\beta)}({\boldsymbol{y}})|+(A/q)^{N-1},

where the second term on the right-hand side accounts for the initial and final interval with length at most
O(1)
for each coordinate of
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
. Meanwhile, since
\frac{d\varphi(y)}{dy}\ll 1
, we note from the chain rule that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\boldsymbol{x}}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\\
{\boldsymbol{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\end{subarray}}\|\nabla w({\boldsymbol{b}},{\boldsymbol{x}})\|\ll\lambda^{-2}+\lambda^{-1},

where we wrote
\nabla=(\partial_{b_{1}},\ldots,\partial_{b_{N}}).

Thus, we deduce from the product rule and the chain rule that the right-hand side in
(\ref{6.124})
is bounded above by

(5.120)

\displaystyle(A/q)^{N}\cdot(q/A(\lambda^{-2}+\lambda^{-1})+q|\beta|A^{k-1})+(A/q)^{N-1}

\displaystyle\ll(A/q)^{N-1}\cdot\lambda^{-2}+(A/q)^{N}\cdot q|\beta|A^{k-1},

where we used
\lambda\leq M_{d,n}=O(1).

Hence, we obtain from
(\ref{6.124})
and the definition of
h_{(\beta)}(\cdot)
that

(5.121)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\boldsymbol{y}}\in B_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\\
{\boldsymbol{y}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}h_{(\beta)}({\boldsymbol{y}})=(A/q)^{N}\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}w(\boldsymbol{\gamma},{\boldsymbol{x}})e(\beta A^{k}P(\boldsymbol{\gamma}))d\boldsymbol{\gamma}+O\bigl(\ (A/q)^{N-1}(\lambda^{-2}+|\beta|A^{k})\bigr).

On substituting
(\ref{6.126})
into
(\ref{6.123})
, it follows that
\Xi_{1}
is seen to be

(5.122)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq q\leq A^{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq a\leq q\\
(q,a)=1)\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq{\boldsymbol{z}}\leq q\\
{\boldsymbol{z}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}(A/q)^{N}e\biggl(\frac{a}{q}P({\boldsymbol{z}})\biggr)\int_{|\beta|\leq\frac{A^{1/(6n)}}{qA^{k}}}I(A^{k}\beta)d\beta+O(E),

where

I(\beta)=\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}w(\boldsymbol{\gamma},{\boldsymbol{x}})e(\beta P(\boldsymbol{\gamma}))d\boldsymbol{\gamma}

and

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq q\leq A^{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq a\leq q\\
(q,a)=1\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq{\boldsymbol{z}}\leq q\\
{\boldsymbol{z}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}e\biggl(\frac{a}{q}P({\boldsymbol{z}})\biggr)\displaystyle\int_{|\beta|\leq\frac{A^{1/(6n)}}{qA^{k}}}(A/q)^{N-1}(\lambda^{-2}+|\beta|A^{k})d\beta

\displaystyle\leq\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq q\leq A^{1/(6n)}}q^{N+1}\cdot\frac{A^{1/(6n)}}{qA^{k}}\cdot(A/q)^{N-1}\left(\lambda^{-2}+\frac{A^{1/(6n)}}{q}\right)\ll\lambda^{-2}A^{N-k-1+1/(2n)}.

Meanwhile, on recalling that

w(\boldsymbol{\gamma},{\boldsymbol{x}})=\varphi\left(\frac{f_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\varphi\left(\frac{\partial_{x_{i}}f_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{2\lambda N}\right),

one finds by the triangle inequality and applying Lemma
2.7
that

I(\beta)\leq\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}|w(\boldsymbol{\gamma},{\boldsymbol{x}})|d\boldsymbol{\gamma}\leq\displaystyle\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\varphi\left(\frac{f_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\varphi\left(\frac{\partial_{x_{i}}f_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\boldsymbol{x}})}{2\lambda N}\right)d\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll\lambda^{n+1},

where, for the last inequality, we used the same treatment leading to the second expression in
(\ref{6.114}).

We next apply the same treatment as applied in the proofs of [
References
, Lemmas 5.2 and 5.4] together with
(\ref{6.124})
and
(\ref{6.126})
using
q=1,a=0
and with the Weyl type estimate over minor arcs for the exponential sum of the integrand in
T(\xi_{0},\boldsymbol{\xi})
leading from
(\ref{6.121})
to
(\ref{6.122})
. Thus, we infer from our choice of
N
that

\int_{|\beta|\leq A^{1/(6n)}}|I(\beta)|^{1/(n+1)}d\beta\ll\int_{|\beta|\leq A^{1/(6n)}}\text{min}\left(1,|\beta|^{-\frac{N}{(n+1)(k-1)2^{k-1}}}\right)d\beta\ll 1

and

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq q\leq A^{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq a\leq q\\
(q,a)=1\end{subarray}}\biggl|\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq{\boldsymbol{z}}\leq q\\
{\boldsymbol{z}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}q^{-N}e\left(\frac{a}{q}P({\boldsymbol{z}})\right)\biggr|\ll 1.

Therefore, by making use of these bounds, we deduce from
(\ref{6.127})
together with the triangle inequality that

(5.123)

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}

\displaystyle\ll A^{N-k}\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq q\leq A^{1/(6n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq a\leq q\\
(q,a)=1\end{subarray}}\biggl|\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq{\boldsymbol{z}}\leq q\\
{\boldsymbol{z}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\end{subarray}}q^{-N}e\left(\frac{a}{q}P({\boldsymbol{z}})\right)\biggr|\displaystyle\int_{|\beta|\leq\frac{A^{1/(6n)}}{q}}|I(\beta)|d\beta+E

\displaystyle\ll A^{N-k}\displaystyle\int_{|\beta|\leq A^{1/(6n)}}|I(\beta)|^{n/(n+1)}|I(\beta)|^{1/(n+1)}d\beta+E

\displaystyle\ll\lambda^{n}A^{N-k}\int_{|\beta|\leq A^{1/(6n)}}|I(\beta)|^{1/(n+1)}d\beta+E\ll\lambda^{n}A^{N-k},

where we have used

E\ll\lambda^{-2}A^{N-k-1+1/(2n)}\ll\lambda^{n}A^{N-k},

obtained from
A^{-1/(2n)}<\lambda
and
n\geq 4.

Therefore, on substituting
(\ref{6.122})
and
(\ref{6.128})
into
(\ref{6.120})
and from that into
(\ref{6.111})
, whenever
A^{-1/(2n)}<\lambda
, we conclude that

(5.124)

\text{mes}\left(B_{N}^{(\lambda)}\right)\ll\lambda A^{-k}.

Hence, combining (
5.107
),
(\ref{6.119})
and
(\ref{6.129}),
we complete the proof of Lemma
\ref{lem6.14}.

∎

The following lemma provides a lower bound for the quantity
\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};\gamma)
for
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in B_{N}^{(\lambda)}.
Furthermore, in the proof of Proposition
5.12
, this lemma plays similar role with that of Lemma
5.11
in the proof of Proposition
\ref{prop6.1}.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
d\geq 2
and
n\geq 4
. Let also
\gamma>0.
For
\lambda\in(0,M_{d,n})
and
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in B_{N}^{(\lambda)}
, we have

\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};\gamma)\gg\lambda^{n}\cdot\min\left\{\gamma,\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\right\}.

Proof.

From
[\ref{ref3},\text{Lemma 5.10}]
, one readily infers that whenever

{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{L}(\lambda)=\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in B_{N}(N)\middle|\ \exists{\boldsymbol{x}}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\ \begin{aligned} &f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})=0\\
&\lambda\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|<\|\nabla f_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|\leq 2\lambda\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|\end{aligned}\right\},

with
\gamma>0
and
\lambda\in(0,M_{d,n})
, we have
\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};\gamma)\gg\lambda^{n}\cdot\text{min}\left\{\gamma,\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\right\}.
Since we obviously have
B_{N}^{(\lambda)}\subseteq\mathcal{L}(\lambda)
, we complete the proof of Lemma
\ref{lem6.1414}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.12
.

As we discussed after the statement of Proposition
\ref{prop6.11},
it suffices to confirm the inequality
(\ref{6.989898})
. For simplicity, we write

\mathfrak{Y}(A)={A^{-N+k}}\cdot\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{A}^{\text{loc}}_{d,n}(A;P)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} C\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};2w^{5}N)\leq(\log A)^{-\eta}\end{aligned}\right\}.

Recall the definition
(\ref{6.99})
of the set
\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}
. Then, we see that

\mathfrak{Y}(A)\ll{A^{-N+k}}\cdot\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\cap[-A,A]^{N}\cap\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}\middle|\ \begin{aligned} C\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};2w^{5}N)&\leq(\log A)^{-\eta}\\
P({\boldsymbol{a}}&)=0\end{aligned}\right\}.

Then, it follows from Lemma
5.13
that

(5.125)

\mathfrak{Y}(A)\ll{A^{-N+k}}\cdot\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\cap[-A,A]^{N}\middle|\ \begin{aligned} \mathcal{N}({\boldsymbol{a}})\subset\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}&,\ P({\boldsymbol{a}})=0\\
C\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};2w^{5}N)&\leq(\log A)^{-\eta}\end{aligned}\right\}+A^{-1/2}.

We now show that if
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
satisfies
\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|\geq 8w^{5}N
then for any
{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathcal{N}({\boldsymbol{a}})
, we have

(5.126)

\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)\leq 2\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};2w^{5}N).

Let
{\bf u}\in B_{n}(1)
be such that

|\langle\nu_{d,n}({\bf u}),{\boldsymbol{y}}\rangle|\leq\frac{\|\nu_{d,n}({\bf u})\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{y}}\|}{4w^{5}N}.

Since
{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathcal{N}({\boldsymbol{a}})
, it follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that
|\langle\nu_{d,n}({\bf u}),{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle|\leq\|\nu_{d,n}({\bf u})\|.
Hence, we find that

|\langle\nu_{d,n}({\bf u}),{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle|\leq\frac{\|\nu_{d,n}({\bf u})\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{y}}\|}{4w^{5}N}+\|\nu_{d,n}({\bf u})\|.

Since we have assumed that
\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|\geq 8w^{5}N,
we have in particular
\|{\boldsymbol{y}}\|\leq\frac{3}{2}\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|.
We deduce that

|\langle\nu_{d,n}({\bf u}),{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle|\leq\frac{3\|\nu_{d,n}({\bf u})\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|}{8w^{5}N}+\|\nu_{d,n}({\bf u})\|.

Then, our assumption
\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|\geq 8w^{5}N
gives

|\langle\nu_{d,n}({\bf u}),{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle|\leq\frac{\|\nu_{d,n}({\bf u})\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|}{2w^{5}N},

which establishes (
5.126
), from the definition of
\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};b).

On recalling the bound
(\ref{6.130})
, we note that the inequality
(\ref{6.131})
gives

(5.127)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Y}(A)

\displaystyle\ll{A^{-N+k}}\cdot\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\cap([-A,A]^{N}\setminus B_{N}(8w^{5}N))\middle|\ \begin{aligned} \mathcal{N}({\boldsymbol{a}})\subset\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}&,\ P({\boldsymbol{a}})=0\\
C\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{a}};2w^{5}N)&\leq(\log A)^{-\eta}\end{aligned}\right\}+A^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\ll{A^{-N+k}}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\cap[-A,A]^{N}\\
P({\boldsymbol{a}})=0\end{subarray}}\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathcal{N}({\boldsymbol{a}})\cap\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}\middle|\ C\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)\leq 2(\log A)^{-\eta}\right\}\right)+A^{-1/2}.

Inverting the summation over
{\boldsymbol{a}}
and the integration over
{\boldsymbol{y}}
and recalling the definition of
C_{P}(A),
the first term in the last display is bounded above by

(5.128)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle A^{-N+k}}\cdot\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{y}}\in B_{N}(NA)\cap\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}\middle|\ \begin{aligned} &C\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)\leq 2(\log A)^{-\eta}\\
&\exists{\boldsymbol{b}}\in[-A,A]^{N}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}\ \text{s.t}\ \left\{\begin{aligned} &\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{b}}\|\leq 1\\
&P({\boldsymbol{b}})=0\end{aligned}\right.\end{aligned}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\ll A^{k}\cdot\text{mes}\left(\left\{{\boldsymbol{y}}\in C_{P}(A)\cap\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}\middle|\ C\cdot\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)\leq 2(\log A)^{-\eta}\right\}\right).

We now use the same trick as in the proof of Proposition
5.1
. On substituting
(\ref{6.133})
into (
5.127
), we deduce that for
\kappa>0

(5.129)

\mathfrak{Y}(A)\ll\frac{A^{k}}{(\log A)^{\eta\cdot\kappa}}\int_{C_{P}(A)\cap\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}}\frac{1}{\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)^{\kappa}}d{\boldsymbol{y}}+A^{-1/2}.

Recall the definition (
5.103
) and
(\ref{6.109})
of
B_{N}^{(\lambda)}
for
\lambda>0
and the quantity
M_{d,n}
. We deduce from the equality
(\ref{6.110})
that

\int_{C_{P}(A)\cap\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}}\frac{1}{\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)^{\kappa}}d{\boldsymbol{y}}\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\int_{B_{N}^{(M_{d,n}/2^{l})}}\frac{1}{\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)^{\kappa}}d{\boldsymbol{y}}.

Let us set
\kappa=1/(2n)
. It follows by applying Lemma
5.14
and Lemma
5.15
that

\displaystyle\int_{C_{P}(A)\cap\mathbb{I}_{d,n}^{\text{loc}}}\frac{1}{\tau({\boldsymbol{y}};4w^{5}N)^{\kappa}}d{\boldsymbol{y}}

\displaystyle\ll\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{2^{l\cdot n}}{w^{5}}+2^{l\cdot(n-2)}\right)^{\kappa}\cdot\text{mes}\left(B_{N}^{(M_{d,n}/2^{l})}\right)

\displaystyle\ll A^{-k}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{w^{5/(2n)}}\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}2^{-l/2}+\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}2^{-l/2-l/n}\right).

On substituting this into
(\ref{6.134})
, we conclude that

\mathfrak{Y}(A)\ll\frac{1}{(\log A)^{\eta/(2n)}}+A^{-1/2}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
\ref{prop6.11}.

∎

6.
Proof of Theorem 1.2

Proof.

We observe that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle A^{-N+k}}\cdot\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{A}^{\text{loc}}_{d,n}(A;P)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} \mathfrak{S}^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathfrak{J}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{*}\leq X^{n-d}A^{-1}(\log A)^{-\eta}\end{aligned}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq{A^{-N+k}}\cdot\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{A}^{\text{loc}}_{d,n}(A;P)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} \text{min}(\mathfrak{S}^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}},AX^{d-n}\mathfrak{J}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{*})\leq(\log A)^{-\eta/2}\end{aligned}\right\}.

Then, on noting that the cardinality of the set in the last expression is bounded above by

\displaystyle\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{A}^{\text{loc}}_{d,n}(A;P)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} \mathfrak{S}^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\leq(\log A)^{-\eta/2}\end{aligned}\right\}+\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{A}^{\text{loc}}_{d,n}(A;P)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} AX^{d-n}\mathfrak{J}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{*}\leq(\log A)^{-\eta/2}\end{aligned}\right\},

it follows by applying Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.12
that

{A^{-N+k}}\cdot\#\left\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{A}^{\text{loc}}_{d,n}(A;P)\middle|\ \begin{aligned} \mathfrak{S}^{*}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathfrak{J}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{*}\leq X^{n-d}A^{-1}(\log A)^{-\eta}\end{aligned}\right\}\ll(\log A)^{-\eta/(40n)}.

∎
